</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Political party</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1151524712">.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin2px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-2px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin3px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-3px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-display-ws{display:inline;white-space:nowrap}</style>
<div class="marriage-display-ws"><div style="display:inline-block;line-height:normal;margin-top:1px;white-space:normal;">Satyamma</div>
   <div class="marriage-line-margin2px">&#8203;</div>&#32;<div style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:1px;">&#8203;</div>&#40;<abbr title="married">m.</abbr>&#160;1931&#59;&#32;died&#160;1970&#41;<wbr />&#8203;</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Children</th><td class="infobox-data">8, including <a href="/wiki/P._V._Rajeshwar_Rao" title="P. mater">Alma mater</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"><div class="plainlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Osmania_University" Mumbai">University of Mumbai</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Nagpur_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Nagpur University">Nagpur University</a> (<a href="/wiki/LL.M." class="mw-redirect" data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1129693374">.mw-parser-output .hlist dl,.mw-parser-output .hlist ol,.mw-parser-output .hlist ul{margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt,.mw-parser-output .hlist li{margin:0;display:inline}.mw-parser-output .hlist.inline,.mw-parser-output .hlist.inline dl,.mw-parser-output .hlist.inline ol,.mw-parser-output .hlist.inline dt::after{content:": "}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist li::after{content:" · ";font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist li:last-child::after{content:none}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd dd:first-child::before,.mw-parser-output .hlist dd dt:first-child::before,.mw-parser-output (";font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd dd:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dd dt:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dd li:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt dd:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt dt:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt li:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist li dd:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist li dt:last-child::after,.mw-parser-output .hlist li li:last-child::after{content:")";font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .hlist ol{counter-reset:listitem}.mw-parser-output .hlist ol>li{counter-increment:listitem}.mw-parser-output .hlist ol>li::before{content:" "counter(listitem)"\a0 "}.mw-parser-output .hlist dd ol>li:first-child::before,.mw-parser-output .hlist dt ol>li:first-child::before,.mw-parser-output .hlist li ol>li:first-child::before{content:" ("counter(listitem)"\a0 "}</style><div class="hlist"><ul><li>Lawyer</li><li>politician</li><li>writer</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pamulaparthi Venkata Narasimha Rao</b> (28 June 1921 – 23 December 2004), popularly known as <b>P. V. Narasimha Rao</b>, was an Indian lawyer, statesman and politician who served as the 9th <a href="/wiki/Prime_minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime minister of India">prime minister of India</a> from 1991 to 1996. He is known for introducing various <a href="/wiki/Economic_liberalisation_in_India" title="Economic liberalisation in India">liberal reforms to India's economy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Narasimha_Rao_was_father_of_economic_reform:_Pranab_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Narasimha_Rao_was_father_of_economic_reform:_Pranab-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> His ascendancy to the prime ministership was politically significant because he was the second holder of this office from a non-Hindi-speaking region and the first from <a href="/wiki/South_India" title="South India">South India</a> (<a href="/wiki/United_Andhra_Pradesh" class="mw-redirect" title="United Andhra Pradesh">United Andhra Pradesh</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup>. He led an important administration, overseeing a major <a href="/wiki/Economic_transformation" title="Economic transformation">economic transformation</a> and several home incidents affecting national security of India.<sup id="cite_ref-Reforming_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Reforming-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Rao, who held the Industries portfolio, was personally responsible for the dismantling of the <a href="/wiki/Licence_Raj" title="Licence Raj">Licence Raj</a>, as this came under the purview of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Commerce_and_Industry_(India)" title="Ministry of Commerce and Industry (India)">Ministry of Commerce and Industry</a>, reversing the economic policies of <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>'s government.<sup id="cite_ref-DNAArticle_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DNAArticle-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Former Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> described Rao as the true <i>father of economic reforms in India</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> In 1991, Rao employed Manmohan Singh as his <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">finance minister</a> to embark on historic economic transition. With Rao's mandate, Manmohan Singh launched India's <a href="/wiki/Globalisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Globalisation">globalisation</a> angle of the reforms that implemented the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> (IMF) policies to rescue the <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_economic_crisis" title="1991 Indian economic crisis">almost bankrupt nation from economic collapse</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DNAArticle_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DNAArticle-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Rao was also referred to as <i><a href="/wiki/Chanakya" title="Chanakya">Chanakya</a></i> for his ability to steer economic and political legislation through the parliament at a time when he headed a <a href="/wiki/Minority_government" title="Minority government">minority government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-frontline_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-frontline-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> 11th <a href="/wiki/President_of_India" title="President of India">president of India</a>, <a href="/wiki/APJ_Abdul_Kalam" class="mw-redirect" title="APJ Abdul Kalam">APJ Abdul Kalam</a> described Rao as a "patriotic statesman who believed that the nation is bigger than the political system". Kalam acknowledged that Rao, had in fact, asked him to get ready for <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_testing" title="Nuclear weapons testing">testing nuclear weapons</a> in 1996, but they were not carried out, due to the change of government pursuant to the 
<a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 Indian general election</a>. The Vajpayee-led <a href="/wiki/National_Democratic_Alliance" title="National Democratic Alliance">NDA</a> government later <a href="/wiki/Pokhran-II" title="Pokhran-II">conducted the nuclear tests </a> in 1998. It emerged later, that Rao had briefed Vajpayee on the state of readiness for nuclear tests, paving the way for this decision.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao's years as prime minister also saw the emergence of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP), a major right-wing party, as an alternative to the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> which had been governing India for most of its post-independence history. Future prime ministers <a href="/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a> and <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> continued the economic reform policies pioneered by Rao's government. Rao died in 2004 of a heart attack in New Delhi. He was cremated in <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad" title="Hyderabad">Hyderabad</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>He was a versatile thinker with interests in a variety of subjects (other than politics) such as literature and computer software (including <a href="/wiki/Computer_programming" title="Computer programming">computer programming</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-The_meek_inheritor_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_meek_inheritor-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He spoke 17 languages.<sup id="cite_ref-PVN_–_Obituary_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PVN_–_Obituary-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-&#39;PV&#39;:_A_scholar,_a_statesman_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-&#39;PV&#39;:_A_scholar,_a_statesman-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Although he was also criticised during his tenure and even sidelined later by his own party,<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> retrospective evaluations have been kinder, even positioning him as one of the best prime ministers of India in various polls and analyses.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> His achievements include steering India through the 1991 economic crisis, completing a tenure with a <a href="/wiki/10th_Lok_Sabha" title="10th Lok Sabha">minority government</a>, establishing <a href="/wiki/India%E2%80%93Israel_relations" title="India–Israel relations">diplomatic relations with Israel</a>, starting India's <a href="/wiki/Look_East_policy_(India)" title="Look East policy (India)">Look East policy</a>, rekindling <a href="/wiki/Indian_nuclear_programme" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian nuclear programme">India's nuclear programme</a>, defeating the 1994 United Nations resolution against India,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> effectively handling and crushing <a href="/wiki/Insurgency_in_Punjab" class="mw-redirect" title="Insurgency in Punjab">insurgency in Punjab</a>, tough policy against <a href="/wiki/Terrorism_in_Kashmir" class="mw-redirect" title="Terrorism in Kashmir">terrorism in Kashmir</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and opening <a href="/wiki/India%E2%80%93Taiwan_relations" title="India–Taiwan relations">partial diplomatic relations with Taiwan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao was born on 28 June 1921 in a <a href="/wiki/Telugu_people" title="Telugu people">Telugu</a> <a href="/wiki/Niyogi_Brahmin" title="Niyogi Brahmin">Niyogi Brahmin</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReddy199335_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReddy199335-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> family in the village of <a href="/wiki/Laknepalli" title="Laknepalli">Laknepalli</a> village of <a href="/wiki/Narsampet" title="Narsampet">Narsampet</a> mandal, <a href="/wiki/Warangal_district" title="Warangal district">Warangal district</a> of present-day <a href="/wiki/Telangana" title="Telangana">Telangana</a> (then part of <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad_State" title="Hyderabad State">Hyderabad State</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReddy199335_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReddy199335-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-thehindu.com_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thehindu.com-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> His father Sitarama Rao and mother Rukma Bai hailed from agrarian families.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Later he was adopted by Pamulaparthi Ranga Rao and Rukminamma and brought to <a href="/wiki/Vangara,_Hanamkonda_district" title="Vangara, Hanamkonda district">Vangara</a>, a village in <a href="/wiki/Bheemadevarpalle" title="Bheemadevarpalle">Bheemadevarpalle</a> mandal of present-day <a href="/wiki/Hanamkonda_district" title="Hanamkonda district">Hanamkonda district</a> in Telangana when he was three years old.<sup id="cite_ref-thehindu.com_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thehindu.com-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESitapati2016_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESitapati2016-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Popularly known as P.&#160;V., he completed part of his primary education in Katkuru village of Bheemdevarapalli mandal in <a href="/wiki/Hanamkonda_district" title="Hanamkonda district">Hanamkonda district</a> by staying in his relative Gabbeta Radhakishan Rao's house and studying for his <a href="/wiki/Bachelor%27s_degree" title="Bachelor&#39;s degree">bachelor's degree</a> in the Arts college at the <a href="/wiki/Osmania_University" title="Osmania University">Osmania University</a>. P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao was part of Vande Mataram movement in the late 1930s in the Hyderabad State. He later went on to <a href="/wiki/Hislop_College" title="Hislop College">Hislop College</a>, now under <a href="/wiki/Nagpur_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Nagpur University">Nagpur University</a>, where he completed a master's degree in law.<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He completed his law from <a href="/wiki/Fergusson_College" title="Fergusson College">Fergusson College</a> in Pune of the University of Bombay (now Mumbai).<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Along with his distant cousin <a href="/wiki/Pamulaparthi_Sadasiva_Rao" title="Pamulaparthi Sadasiva Rao">Pamulaparthi Sadasiva Rao</a>, Ch. Raja Narendra and Devulapalli Damodar Rao, P.&#160;V. edited a Telugu weekly magazine called <i>Kakatiya Patrika</i> in the 1940s.<sup id="cite_ref-kakatiya1_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kakatiya1-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Both P.&#160;V. and Sadasiva Rao contributed articles under the pen-name <i>Jaya-Vijaya</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-kakatiya1_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kakatiya1-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-kakatiya2_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kakatiya2-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> He served as the Chairman of the Telugu Academy in Andhra Pradesh from 1968 to 1974.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_career">Political career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Political career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao,_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs,_to_the_CEC.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg/220px-Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg/330px-Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg/440px-Visit_of_Narasimha_Rao%2C_Indian_Minister_for_Foreign_Affairs%2C_to_the_CEC.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2244" data-file-height="2244" /></a><figcaption>Visit of Narasimha Rao, Indian Minister for Foreign Affairs, to the CEC</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rao was an active freedom fighter during the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Independence_movement" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian Independence movement">Indian Independence movement</a><sup id="cite_ref-Embassy_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Embassy-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> and joined full-time politics after independence as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He served as an elected representative for Andhra Pradesh State Assembly from 1957 to 1977.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He served in various ministerial positions in Andhra government from 1962 to 1973.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He became the <a href="/wiki/Chief_minister_of_Andhra_Pradesh" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief minister of Andhra Pradesh">Chief minister of Andhra Pradesh</a> in 1971 and implemented land reforms and land ceiling acts strictly.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He secured reservation for lower castes in politics during his tenure.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> President's rule had to be imposed to counter the <a href="/wiki/Jai_Andhra_movement" class="mw-redirect" title="Jai Andhra movement">Jai Andhra movement</a> during his tenure.<sup id="cite_ref-Telegraph_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Telegraph-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He supported <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> in formation of New Congress party in 1969 by splitting the Indian National Congress.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> This was later regrouped as Congress (I) party in 1978.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He served as Member of Parliament, Lok Sabha from Andhra Pradesh.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He rose to national prominence for handling several diverse portfolios, most significantly <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">Home</a>, <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(India)" title="Minister of Defence (India)">Defence</a> and <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (India)">Foreign Affairs</a>, in the cabinets of both Indira Gandhi and <a href="/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Rajiv Gandhi">Rajiv Gandhi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He served as Foreign minister from 1980 to 1984 and then from 1988 to 1989.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In fact, it is speculated that he was in the running for the post of <a href="/wiki/President_of_India" title="President of India">India's President</a> along with <a href="/wiki/Zail_Singh" title="Zail Singh">Zail Singh</a> in 1982.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (April 2020)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Rao very nearly retired from politics in 1991. He was Indian National Congress President  from 29 May' 1991- Sept.1996. It was the <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Rajiv_Gandhi" title="Assassination of Rajiv Gandhi">assassination of the Congress President Rajiv Gandhi</a> that persuaded him to make a comeback.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> As the Congress had won the largest number of seats in the <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_general_election#1991" title="1991 Indian general election">1991 elections</a>, he had an opportunity to head the <a href="/wiki/Minority_government" title="Minority government">minority government</a> as Prime Minister. He was the first person outside the <a href="/wiki/Nehru%E2%80%93Gandhi_family" title="Nehru–Gandhi family">Nehru–Gandhi family</a> to serve as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister</a> for five continuous years, the first to hail from the State of <a href="/wiki/Telangana" title="Telangana">Telangana</a><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup>, and also the first from <a href="/wiki/Southern_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Southern India">Southern India</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Reforming_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Reforming-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Since Rao had not contested the general elections, he then participated in a by-election in <a href="/wiki/Nandyal_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Nandyal (Lok Sabha constituency)">Nandyal</a> to join the parliament. Rao won from Nandyal with a victory margin of a record 5 lakh (500,000) votes and his win was recorded in the <a href="/wiki/Guinness_Book_Of_World_Records" class="mw-redirect" title="Guinness Book Of World Records">Guinness Book Of World Records</a>; later on, in 1996, he was MP from Berhampur, Ganjam District, Odisha.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> His cabinet included <a href="/wiki/Sharad_Pawar" title="Sharad Pawar">Sharad Pawar</a>, himself a strong contender for the Prime Minister's post, as <a href="/wiki/Defence_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Defence Ministers of India">Defence Minister</a>. He also broke a convention by appointing a non-political economist and future prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> as his <a href="/wiki/Finance_Minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Finance Minister of India">Finance Minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cabinet1_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cabinet1-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cabinet2_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cabinet2-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> He also appointed <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a>, an opposition party (<a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>) member as the Chairman of the Commission on Labour Standards and International Trade. This has been the only instance that an opposition party member was given a Cabinet rank post by the ruling party. He also sent opposition leader <a href="/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a>, to represent India in a UN meeting at Geneva.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Narasimha Rao fought and won elections from different parts of India such as <a href="/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh" title="Andhra Pradesh">Andhra Pradesh</a>, Maharashtra and <a href="/wiki/Odisha" title="Odisha">Odisha</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ramtek_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ramtek-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-orissa_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-orissa-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
