<h2><span id="Prime_minister_.281991-1996.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_minister_(1991-1996)">Prime minister (1991-1996)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Prime minister (1991-1996)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_reforms">Economic reforms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Economic reforms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economic_liberalisation_in_India" title="Economic liberalisation in India">Economic liberalisation in India</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_economic_crisis" title="1991 Indian economic crisis">1991 Indian economic crisis</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif/lossy-page1-220px-Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif/lossy-page1-330px-Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif/lossy-page1-440px-Pamulaparti_Venkata_Narasimha_Rao_Addressing_-_Inaugural_Function_-_National_Science_Centre_-_New_Delhi_1992-01-09_247.tif.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5654" data-file-height="3714" /></a><figcaption>Rao addressing the inaugural function at National Science Centre, New Delhi in 1992</figcaption></figure>
<p>Adopted to avert the impending <a href="/wiki/1991_Indian_economic_crisis" title="1991 Indian economic crisis">1991 economic crisis</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Narasimha_Rao_was_father_of_economic_reform:_Pranab_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Narasimha_Rao_was_father_of_economic_reform:_Pranab-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghosh_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghosh-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> the reforms progressed furthest in the areas of opening up to <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign investment</a>, reforming <a href="/wiki/Capital_markets" class="mw-redirect" title="Capital markets">capital markets</a>, <a href="/wiki/Deregulation" title="Deregulation">deregulating</a> domestic business, and reforming the trade regime. Rao's government's goals were reducing the <a href="/wiki/Fiscal_deficit" class="mw-redirect" title="Fiscal deficit">fiscal deficit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Privatisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Privatisation">privatisation</a> of the public sector and increasing investment in infrastructure. Trade reforms and changes in the regulation of <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a> were introduced to open India to foreign trade while stabilising external loans. Rao wanted <a href="/wiki/I._G._Patel" title="I. G. Patel">I.&#160;G. Patel</a> as his <a href="/wiki/Finance_Minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Finance Minister of India">Finance Minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Time_To_Tune_In_To_FM_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Time_To_Tune_In_To_FM-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Patel was an official who helped prepare 14 budgets, an ex-governor of the <a href="/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India" title="Reserve Bank of India">Reserve Bank of India</a> and had headed <a href="/wiki/The_London_School_of_Economics" class="mw-redirect" title="The London School of Economics">The London School of Economics</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Time_To_Tune_In_To_FM_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Time_To_Tune_In_To_FM-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> But Patel declined. Rao then chose <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> for the job. Manmohan Singh, an acclaimed economist, played a central role in implementing these reforms. 
</p><p>Major reforms in India's capital markets led to an influx of foreign portfolio investment. The major economic policies adopted by Rao include:
</p>
<ul><li>Abolishing in 1992 the Controller of Capital Issues which decided the prices and number of shares that firms could issue.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghosh_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghosh-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Introducing the SEBI Act of 1992 and the Security Laws (Amendment) which gave <a href="/wiki/Securities_and_Exchange_Board_of_India" title="Securities and Exchange Board of India">SEBI</a> the legal authority to register and regulate all security market intermediaries.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghosh_47-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghosh-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Opening up in 1992 of India's <a href="/wiki/Stock_market" title="Stock market">equity markets</a> to investment by foreign institutional investors and permitting Indian firms to raise capital on international markets by issuing <a href="/wiki/Global_Depository_Receipt" class="mw-redirect" title="Global Depository Receipt">Global Depository Receipts</a> (GDRs).<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Starting in 1994 of the <a href="/wiki/National_Stock_Exchange_of_India" title="National Stock Exchange of India">National Stock Exchange</a> as a computer-based trading system which served as an instrument to leverage reforms of India's other stock exchanges. The NSE emerged as India's largest exchange by 1996.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Reducing tariffs from an average of 85 per cent to 25 per cent, and rolling back quantitative controls. (The rupee was made convertible on trade account.)<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Encouraging foreign direct investment by increasing the maximum limit on share of foreign capital in joint ventures from 40 to 51% with 100% foreign equity permitted in priority sectors.<sup id="cite_ref-Asiaweek_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Asiaweek-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Streamlining procedures for FDI approvals, and in at least 35 industries, automatically approving projects within the limits for foreign participation.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghosh_47-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghosh-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>The impact of these reforms may be gauged from the fact that total foreign investment (including foreign direct investment, portfolio investment, and investment raised on international capital markets) in India grew from a minuscule US$132&#160;million in 1991–92 to $5.3&#160;billion in 1995–96.<sup id="cite_ref-Asiaweek_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Asiaweek-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Rao began industrial policy reforms with the manufacturing sector. He slashed industrial <a href="/wiki/Licensing" class="mw-redirect" title="Licensing">licensing</a>, leaving only 18 industries subject to licensing. Industrial regulation was rationalised.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghosh_47-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghosh-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="National_security.2C_foreign_policy_and_crisis_management"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="National_security,_foreign_policy_and_crisis_management">National security, foreign policy and crisis management</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: National security, foreign policy and crisis management">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Rabiya.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Rabiya.jpg/220px-Rabiya.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Rabiya.jpg/330px-Rabiya.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Rabiya.jpg/440px-Rabiya.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="800" /></a><figcaption>P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao at an awards function, 1993</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rao energised the national <a href="/wiki/India_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction" title="India and weapons of mass destruction">nuclear security</a> and <a href="/wiki/Integrated_Guided_Missile_Development_Programme" title="Integrated Guided Missile Development Programme">ballistic missiles</a> programme, which ultimately resulted in the <a href="/wiki/Pokhran-II" title="Pokhran-II">1998 Pokhran nuclear tests</a>. It is speculated that the tests were actually planned in 1995, during Rao's term in office,<sup id="cite_ref-Bomb_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bomb-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> and that they were dropped under American pressure when the US intelligence got the whiff of it.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Another view was that he purposefully leaked the information to gain time to develop and test thermonuclear device which was not yet ready.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> He increased military spending, and set the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Army" title="Indian Army">Indian Army</a> on course to fight the emerging threat of terrorism and insurgencies, as well as Pakistan and China's nuclear potentials. It was during his term that khalistani terrorism in the Indian state of <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_India" title="Punjab, India">Punjab</a> was finally defeated.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Also scenarios of <a href="/wiki/Aircraft_hijacking" title="Aircraft hijacking">aircraft hijackings</a>, which occurred during Rao's time ended without the government conceding the terrorists' demands.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> He also directed negotiations to secure the release of Doraiswamy, an <a href="/wiki/Indian_Oil_Corporation" title="Indian Oil Corporation">Indian Oil</a> executive, from <a href="/wiki/Kashmiri_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Kashmiri people">Kashmiri</a> terrorists who kidnapped him,<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> and Liviu Radu, a Romanian diplomat posted in New Delhi in October 1991, who was kidnapped by Sikh terrorists.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Rao also handled the Indian response to the occupation of the <a href="/wiki/Hazratbal" title="Hazratbal">Hazratbal</a> holy shrine in <a href="/wiki/Jammu_and_Kashmir_(state)" title="Jammu and Kashmir (state)">Jammu and Kashmir</a> by terrorists in October 1993.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> He brought the occupation to an end without damage to the shrine. Similarly, he dealt with the kidnapping of some foreign tourists by a terrorist group called <i>Al Faran</i> in Kashmir valley in 1995 effectively. Although he could not secure the release of the hostages, his policies ensured that the terrorists demands were not conceded to, and that the action of the terrorists was condemned internationally, including Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao also made diplomatic overtures to Western Europe, the United States, and China.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> He decided in 1992 to bring into the open India's relations with Israel, which had been kept covertly active for a few years during his tenure as a Foreign Minister, and permitted Israel to open an embassy in New Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> He ordered the intelligence community in 1992 to start a systematic drive to draw the international community's attention to Pakistan's <a href="/wiki/State_terrorism" title="State terrorism">sponsorship of terrorism</a> against India and not to be discouraged by US efforts to undermine the exercise.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Rao launched the <i><a href="/wiki/Look_East_policy_(India)" title="Look East policy (India)">Look East</a></i> foreign policy, which brought India closer to <a href="/wiki/ASEAN" title="ASEAN">ASEAN</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Rejaul_Karim_Laskar" title="Rejaul Karim Laskar">Rejaul Karim Laskar</a>, a scholar of India's foreign policy and ideologue of Rao's <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress Party</a>, Rao initiated the <a href="/wiki/Look_East_policy_(India)" title="Look East policy (India)">Look East policy</a> with three objectives in mind, namely, to renew political contacts with the ASEAN-member nation; to increase economic interaction with South East Asia in trade, investment, science and technology, tourism, etc.; and to forge strategic and defence links with several countries of South East Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> He decided to maintain a distance from the <a href="/wiki/Dalai_Lama" title="Dalai Lama">Dalai Lama</a> in order to avoid aggravating Beijing's suspicions and concerns, and made successful overtures to <a href="/wiki/Tehran" title="Tehran">Tehran</a>. The 'cultivate <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Iran</a>' policy was pushed through vigorously by him.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> These policies paid rich dividends for India in March 1994, when <a href="/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto" title="Benazir Bhutto">Benazir Bhutto</a>'s efforts to have a resolution passed by the <a href="/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Commission" class="mw-redirect" title="UN Human Rights Commission">UN Human Rights Commission</a> in Geneva on the human rights situation in Jammu and Kashmir failed, with opposition by China and Iran.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao's crisis management after 12 March <a href="/wiki/1993_Bombay_bombings" title="1993 Bombay bombings">1993 Bombay bombings</a> was highly praised. He personally visited Bombay after the blasts and after seeing evidence of Pakistani involvement in the blasts, ordered the intelligence community to invite the intelligence agencies of the US, UK and other West European countries to send their counter-terrorism experts to Bombay to examine the facts for themselves.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_crisis_and_initiation_of_liberalisation">Economic crisis and initiation of liberalisation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Economic crisis and initiation of liberalisation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Rao decided that India, which in 1991 was on the brink of bankruptcy,<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> would benefit from <a href="/wiki/Economic_liberalization" title="Economic liberalization">liberalising</a> its economy. He appointed economist Manmohan Singh, a former governor of the <a href="/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India" title="Reserve Bank of India">Reserve Bank of India</a>, as <a href="/wiki/Finance_Minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Finance Minister of India">Finance Minister</a> to accomplish his goals.<sup id="cite_ref-Reforming_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Reforming-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> This liberalisation was criticised by many socialist nationalists at that time.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He is often referred as 'Father of Indian Economic Reforms'.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> PV Narasimha Rao: The 10th Prime Minister who changed the face of Indian economy under Rao's mandate and leadership, then finance minister Manmohan Singh launched a series of pro-globalisation reforms, including International Monetary Fund (IMF) policies, to rescue the almost-bankrupt nation from economic collapse.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Father_of_Indian_nuclear_programme">Father of Indian nuclear programme</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Father of Indian nuclear programme">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kalam recalls that Rao ordered him not to test, since "the election result was quite different from what he anticipated". The BJP's <a href="/wiki/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a> took over as prime minister on 16 May 1996. Narasimha Rao, Abdul Kalam and R Chidambaram went to meet the new prime minister "so that", in Kalam's telling, "the smooth takeover of such a very important programme can take place".<sup id="cite_ref-scroll.in_810874_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scroll.in_810874-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao knew he had only one chance to test before sanctions kicked in, i.e., he could not both test conventional atomic bombs in December 1995 as well as the hydrogen bomb separately in April 1996. As <a href="/wiki/Shekhar_Gupta" title="Shekhar Gupta">Shekhar Gupta</a> – who has had unprecedented access to Rao as well as the nuclear team – speculates: "By late 1995, Rao's scientists told him that they needed six more months. They could test some weapons but not others...thermonuclear etc. So Rao began a charade of taking preliminary steps to test, without intending to test then."
</p><p>National elections were scheduled for May 1996, and Rao spent the next two months campaigning. On 8 May at 21:00, Abdul Kalam was asked to immediately meet with the prime minister. Rao told him, "Kalam, be ready with the Department of Atomic Energy and your team for the N-test and I am going to Tirupati. You wait for my authorisation to go ahead with the test. DRDO-DAE teams must be ready for action."
Rao energised the national nuclear security and ballistic missiles programme. His efforts resulted in the 1998 Pokhran nuclear tests.
</p><p>Rao was the "true father" of India's nuclear programme. Vajpayee said that, in May 1996, a few days after he had succeeded Rao as prime minister, "Rao told me that the bomb was ready. I only exploded it."
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_releasing_an_onerupee_commemorative_coin_on_late_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_former_prime_Minister,_on_the_occasion_of_his_first_death_anniversary_in_New_Delhi_on_May_21,_1992.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_releasing_an_onerupee_commemorative_coin_on_late_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_former_prime_Minister%2C_on_the_occasion_of_his_first_death_anniversary_in_New_Delhi_on_May_21%2C_1992.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_releasing_an_onerupee_commemorative_coin_on_late_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_former_prime_Minister%2C_on_the_occasion_of_his_first_death_anniversary_in_New_Delhi_on_May_21%2C_1992.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_releasing_an_onerupee_commemorative_coin_on_late_Shri_Rajiv_Gandhi_former_prime_Minister%2C_on_the_occasion_of_his_first_death_anniversary_in_New_Delhi_on_May_21%2C_1992.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="622" /></a><figcaption>The Prime Minister Shri P.V. Narasimha Rao releasing an onerupee commemorative coin on late Shri Rajiv Gandhi former prime Minister, on the occasion of his first death anniversary in New Delhi on 21 May 1992</figcaption></figure>
<p>"Saamagri tayyar hai," Rao had said. ("The ingredients are ready.") "You can go ahead."
The conventional narrative at the time was that prime minister Rao had wanted to test nuclear weapons in December 1995. The Americans had caught on, and Rao had dithered – as was his wont. Three years later, prime minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee fulfilled his party's campaign promise by ordering five nuclear tests below the shimmering sands of Rajasthan.<sup id="cite_ref-scroll.in_810874_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scroll.in_810874-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Handling_of_separatist_movements">Handling of separatist movements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Handling of separatist movements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Separatist_movements_of_India" title="Separatist movements of India">Separatist movements of India</a></div>
<p>Rao successfully decimated the Sikh separatist movement and neutralised Kashmiri separatist movement to cetain extent. It is said that Rao was 'solely responsible' for the decision to hold elections in Punjab, no matter how narrow the electorate base would be.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In dealing with Kashmir Rao's government was highly restrained by US government and its president Bill Clinton. Rao's government introduced the <a href="/wiki/Terrorist_and_Disruptive_Activities_(Prevention)_Act" title="Terrorist and Disruptive Activities (Prevention) Act">Terrorist and Disruptive Activities (Prevention) Act</a> (TADA),<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> India's first anti-terrorism legislation, and directed the Indian Army to eliminate the infiltrators from Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-Yale_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Yale-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Despite a heavy and largely successful Army campaign, Pakistani Media accuses that the state descended into a security nightmare. Tourism and commerce were also largely disrupted.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Babri_Mosque_riots">Babri Mosque riots</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Babri Mosque riots">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya_debate" class="mw-redirect" title="Ayodhya debate">Ayodhya debate</a></div>
<p>In the late 1980s, the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP) brought the <a href="/wiki/Ram_Janmabhoomi" title="Ram Janmabhoomi">Ram Janmabhoomi</a> issue to the centre stage of national politics, and the BJP and VHP began organising larger protests in Ayodhya and around the country.
</p><p>Members of the <a href="/wiki/Vishva_Hindu_Parishad" title="Vishva Hindu Parishad">Vishva Hindu Parishad</a> (VHP) demolished the <a href="/wiki/Babri_Mosque" class="mw-redirect" title="Babri Mosque">Babri Mosque</a> (which was constructed by Mir Baqi, a general of India's first Mughal Emperor, Babur<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>) in <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya" title="Ayodhya">Ayodhya</a> on 6 December 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> The site is believed to be the birthplace of the Hindu god <a href="/wiki/Rama" title="Rama">Rama</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The destruction of the disputed structure, which was widely reported in the international media, unleashed large scale communal violence, the most extensive since the <a href="/wiki/Partition_of_India" title="Partition of India">Partition of India</a>. Hindus and Muslims were indulged in massive rioting across the country, and almost every major city including Delhi, Mumbai, <a href="/wiki/Kolkata" title="Kolkata">Kolkata</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ahmedabad" title="Ahmedabad">Ahmedabad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad,_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Hyderabad, India">Hyderabad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bhopal" title="Bhopal">Bhopal</a> struggled to control the unrest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEP._V._Narasimha_Rao200658_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEP._V._Narasimha_Rao200658-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Later <a href="/wiki/Liberhan_Commission" title="Liberhan Commission">Liberhan Commission</a>, after extensive hearing and investigation, exonerated P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao. It pointed out that Rao was heading a minority government, the Commission accepted the centre's submission that central forces could neither be deployed by the Union in the totality of facts and circumstances then prevailing, nor could President's Rule be imposed "on the basis of rumours or media reports". Taking such a step would have created "bad precedent" damaging the federal structure and would have "amounted to interference" in the state administration, it said. The state "deliberately and consciously understated" the risk to the disputed structure and general law and order. It also said that the Governor's assessment of the situation was either badly flawed or overly optimistic and was thus a major impediment for the central government. The Commission further said, "... knowing fully well that its facetious undertakings before the Supreme Court had bought it sufficient breathing space, it (state government) proceeded with the planning for the destruction of the disputed structure. The Supreme Court's own observer failed to alert it to the sinister undercurrents. The Governor and its intelligence agencies, charged with acting as the eyes and ears of the central government also failed in their task. Without substantive procedural prerequisites, neither the Supreme Court, nor the Union of India was able to take any meaningful steps."<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In yet another discussion with journalist <a href="/wiki/Shekhar_Gupta" title="Shekhar Gupta">Shekhar Gupta</a>, Rao answered several of the questions on the demolition. He said he was wary of the impact of hundreds of deaths on the nation, and it could have been far worse. And also he had to consider the scenario in which some of the troops might have turned around and joined the mobs instead. Regarding dismissal of Kalyan Singh (government), he said, "mere dismissal does not mean you can take control. It takes a day or so appointing advisers, sending them to Lucknow, taking control of the state. Meanwhile, what had to happen would have happened and there would have been no Kalyan Singh to blame either."<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Latur_earthquake">Latur earthquake</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Latur earthquake">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1993_Latur_earthquake" title="1993 Latur earthquake">1993 Latur earthquake</a></div>
<p>In 1993, a <a href="/wiki/1993_Latur_earthquake" title="1993 Latur earthquake">strong earthquake in Latur</a>, Maharashtra killed nearly 10,000 people and displaced hundreds of thousands.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Rao was applauded by many for using modern technology and resources to organise major relief operations to assuage the stricken people, and for schemes of <a href="/wiki/Economic_reconstruction" title="Economic reconstruction">economic reconstruction</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Purulia_arms_drop_case">Purulia arms drop case</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Purulia arms drop case">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Purulia_arms_drop_case" title="Purulia arms drop case">Purulia arms drop case</a></div>
<p>Narasimha Rao was charged for his facilitating safe exit of accused of 1995 Purulia arms drop case.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Although, it was never proved.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Corruption_charges_and_acquittal">Corruption charges and acquittal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Corruption charges and acquittal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the early 1990s, one of the earliest accusations came in the form of stockbroker <a href="/wiki/Harshad_Mehta" title="Harshad Mehta">Harshad Mehta</a>, who through his lawyer, <a href="/wiki/Ram_Jethmalani" title="Ram Jethmalani">Ram Jethmalani</a>, revealed that he had paid a sum of one crore rupees to the then prime minister Rao for help in closing his cases.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao's government faced a <a href="/wiki/No-confidence_motion" class="mw-redirect" title="No-confidence motion">no-confidence motion</a> in July 1993, because the opposition felt that it did not have sufficient numbers to prove a majority. It was alleged that Rao, through a representative, offered millions of rupees to members of the <a href="/wiki/Jharkhand_Mukti_Morcha" title="Jharkhand Mukti Morcha">Jharkhand Mukti Morcha</a> (JMM), and possibly a breakaway faction of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Dal" title="Janata Dal">Janata Dal</a>, to vote for him during the confidence motion. <a href="/wiki/Shailendra_Mahato" title="Shailendra Mahato">Shailendra Mahato</a>, one of those members who had accepted the bribe, turned <a href="/wiki/Approver" class="mw-redirect" title="Approver">approver</a>. In 1996, after Rao's term in office had expired, investigations began in earnest in the case. In 2000, after years of legal proceedings, a special court convicted Rao and his colleague, <a href="/wiki/Buta_Singh" title="Buta Singh">Buta Singh</a> (who is alleged to have escorted the MPs to the Prime Minister).<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Rao was sentenced to three years in prison for corruption. "I sentence the accused PV Narasimha Rao and Buta Singh to rigorous imprisonment up to three years and a fine of 100,000 rupees ($2,150)," the judge said in his order.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Rao appealed to the <a href="/wiki/Delhi_High_Court" title="Delhi High Court">Delhi High Court</a> and remained free on bail. In 2002, the Delhi High Court overturned the lower court's decision mainly due to the doubt in credibility of Mahato's statements (which were extremely inconsistent) and both Rao and Buta Singh were cleared of the charges.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rao, along with fellow minister K.&#160;K. Tewary, <a href="/wiki/Chandraswami" title="Chandraswami">Chandraswami</a> and K.&#160;N. Aggarwal, were accused of forging documents showing that Ajeya Singh had opened a bank account in the First Trust Corporation Bank in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Kitts" title="Saint Kitts">Saint Kitts</a> and deposited $21&#160;million in it, making his father <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V.&#160;P. Singh</a> its beneficiary. The alleged intent was to tarnish V.&#160;P. Singh's image. This supposedly happened in 1989. However, only after Rao's term as PM had expired in 1996, was he formally charged by the <a href="/wiki/Central_Bureau_of_Investigation" title="Central Bureau of Investigation">Central Bureau of Investigation</a> (CBI) for the crime. Less than a year later the court acquitted him due to lack of evidence linking him with the case.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Lakhubhai Pathak, an Indian businessman living in England, alleged that <a href="/wiki/Chandraswami" title="Chandraswami">Chandraswami</a> and K.&#160;N. Aggarwal alias Mamaji, along with Rao, cheated him out of $100,000. The amount was given for an express promise for allowing supplies of paper pulp in India, and Pathak alleged that he spent an additional $30,000 entertaining Chandraswami and his secretary. Narasimha Rao and Chandraswami were acquitted of the charges in 2003 and before his death, Rao was acquitted of all the cases charged against him.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_life_and_financial_difficulties">Later life and financial difficulties</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Later life and financial difficulties">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In spite of significant achievements in a difficult situation, in the <a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 general elections</a> the Indian electorate voted out Rao's Congress Party. Soon, Sonia Gandhi's supporters forced Mr. Rao to step down as Party President.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Sitaram_Kesri" title="Sitaram Kesri">Sitaram Kesri</a>.
</p><p>Rao rarely spoke of his personal views and opinions during his 5-year tenure. After his retirement from national politics, he published a novel called <a href="/wiki/The_Insider_(Rao_novel)" title="The Insider (Rao novel)">The Insider</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEP_V_N_Rao2000_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEP_V_N_Rao2000-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The book, which follows a man's rise through the ranks of Indian politics, resembled events from Rao's own life.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Mauritius_Prime_Minister,_Mr._Anerood_Jugannath_greeting_the_Union_Finance_Minister_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_at_the_dinner_hosted_in_the_former%27s_honour,_by_Prime_Minister_Shri_P._V._Narasimha_New_Delhi_on_July_24,_1991.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/The_Mauritius_Prime_Minister%2C_Mr._Anerood_Jugannath_greeting_the_Union_Finance_Minister_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_at_the_dinner_hosted_in_the_former%27s_honour%2C_by_Prime_Minister_Shri_P._V._Narasimha_New_Delhi_on_July_24%2C_1991.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/The_Mauritius_Prime_Minister%2C_Mr._Anerood_Jugannath_greeting_the_Union_Finance_Minister_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_at_the_dinner_hosted_in_the_former%27s_honour%2C_by_Prime_Minister_Shri_P._V._Narasimha_New_Delhi_on_July_24%2C_1991.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/The_Mauritius_Prime_Minister%2C_Mr._Anerood_Jugannath_greeting_the_Union_Finance_Minister_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_at_the_dinner_hosted_in_the_former%27s_honour%2C_by_Prime_Minister_Shri_P._V._Narasimha_New_Delhi_on_July_24%2C_1991.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="577" /></a><figcaption>The Mauritius Prime Minister, Mr. Anerood Jugannath greeting the Union Finance Minister Manmohan Singh at the dinner hosted in the former's honour, by Prime Minister Shri P. V. Narasimha New Delhi on 24 July 1991</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to a vernacular source, despite holding many influential posts in Government, he faced many financial troubles. One of his sons was educated with the assistance of his son-in-law. He also faced trouble paying fees for a daughter who was studying medicine.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/P._V._R._K._Prasad" title="P. V. R. K. Prasad">P.&#160;V.&#160;R.&#160;K. Prasad</a>, an <a href="/wiki/Indian_Administrative_Service" title="Indian Administrative Service">Indian Administrative Service</a> (IAS) officer who was Narasimha Rao's media advisor when the latter was Prime Minister, Rao asked his friends to sell away his house at <a href="/wiki/Banjara_Hills" title="Banjara Hills">Banjara Hills</a> to clear the dues of lawyers.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Army,_Navy_%26_Air_Force_personnels,_the_three_wings_of_services,_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Late_Shri_P_V_Narasimha_Rao_towards_service_aircraft_at_Palam_Airport,_New_Delhi_on_December_24.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Army%2C_Navy_%26_Air_Force_personnels%2C_the_three_wings_of_services%2C_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P_V_Narasimha_Rao_towards_service_aircraft_at_Palam_Airport%2C_New_Delhi_on_December_24.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Army%2C_Navy_%26_Air_Force_personnels%2C_the_three_wings_of_services%2C_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P_V_Narasimha_Rao_towards_service_aircraft_at_Palam_Airport%2C_New_Delhi_on_December_24.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Army%2C_Navy_%26_Air_Force_personnels%2C_the_three_wings_of_services%2C_carrying_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P_V_Narasimha_Rao_towards_service_aircraft_at_Palam_Airport%2C_New_Delhi_on_December_24.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1258" /></a><figcaption>The three wings of India's military services, carrying the mortal remains of P.V. Narshima Rao towards service aircraft at Palam Airport, New Delhi on 24 December 2004 for onward journey to Hyderabad where cremation was performed.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tribute_to_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Late_Shri_P.V._Narsimha_Rao_on_his_3rd_death_anniversary,_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23,_2007.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tribute_to_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P.V._Narsimha_Rao_on_his_3rd_death_anniversary%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2007.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tribute_to_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P.V._Narsimha_Rao_on_his_3rd_death_anniversary%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2007.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Dr._Manmohan_Singh_paying_floral_tribute_to_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Late_Shri_P.V._Narsimha_Rao_on_his_3rd_death_anniversary%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2007.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1541" /></a><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_President,_Dr._A.P.J._Abdul_Kalam_paying_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Late_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23,_2004.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/The_President%2C_Dr._A.P.J._Abdul_Kalam_paying_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Late_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2004.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/The_President%2C_Dr._A.P.J._Abdul_Kalam_paying_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Late_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2004.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/The_President%2C_Dr._A.P.J._Abdul_Kalam_paying_tributes_at_the_mortal_remains_of_the_former_Prime_Minister_Late_Shri_P.V._Narasimha_Rao_in_New_Delhi_on_December_23%2C_2004.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1900" data-file-height="1255" /></a><figcaption>The President, A.P.J. Abdul Kalam paying tributes at the mortal remains of the former Prime Minister Late Shri P.V. Narasimha Rao in New Delhi on 23 December 2004</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rao suffered a heart attack on 9 December 2004, and was taken to the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Institute_of_Medical_Sciences" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Institute of Medical Sciences">All India Institute of Medical Sciences</a> where he died 14 days later at the age of 83.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> His family wanted the body cremated in Delhi. "This is his karmabhoomi", Rao's son Prabhakara told Manmohan Singh. But it is alleged that Sonia Gandhi's closest aide <a href="/wiki/Ahmed_Patel" title="Ahmed Patel">Ahmed Patel</a> and others ensured that the body was moved to Hyderabad.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In Delhi, his body was not allowed inside <a href="/wiki/All_India_Congress_Committee" title="All India Congress Committee">AICC</a> building.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> His body was kept in state at the Jubilee Hall in <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad,_Andhra_Pradesh" class="mw-redirect" title="Hyderabad, Andhra Pradesh">Hyderabad</a>. His funeral was attended by the Prime Minister of India <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a>, the Home Affairs Minister <a href="/wiki/Shivraj_Patil" title="Shivraj Patil">Shivraj Patil</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP) president <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">L.&#160;K. Advani</a>, the Defence Minister <a href="/wiki/Pranab_Mukherjee" title="Pranab Mukherjee">Pranab Mukherjee</a>, the Finance Minister <a href="/wiki/P._Chidambaram" title="P. Chidambaram">P. Chidambaram</a> and many other dignitaries. Rao was a long-time widower, since his wife died in 1970 and he was survived by his eight children.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> A memorial was built for P. V. Narasimha Rao located adjacent to <a href="/wiki/Sanjeevaiah_Park" title="Sanjeevaiah Park">Sanjeevaiah Park</a>, developed in 2005 on 1.2 hectares (2.9 acres) of land known as Gyan Bhumi.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The Government of Telangana declared his birthday to be celebrated as a Telangana State function in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> 10 years after his death, P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao was accorded a memorial in Delhi at Ekta Sthal, which is now integrated with Rashtriya Smriti, a common place for erecting memorials for former Presidents, PMs and others. The memorial is raised on a plinth in marble bearing text highlighting briefly his contributions. The plaque describes Rao: "Known as the scholar Prime Minister of India, Shri P V Narasimha Rao was born on 28 June 1921 in Vangara, Karimnagar District in Telangana state. He rose to prominence as freedom fighter who fought the misrule of the Nizam during the formative years of his political career. A reformer, educationist, scholar, conversant in 15 languages and known for his intellectual contribution, he was called the 'Brihaspati' (wiseman) of Andhra Pradesh."<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1931, the 10-year-old Narasimha Rao was married to Satyamma, a girl of his own age, belonging to his own community and coming from a family of similar background.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The marriage, which was arranged by their families in the usual Indian way, was entirely harmonious and it lasted all their lives. Smt. Satyamma died on 1 July 1970. 
</p><p>The couple had three sons and five daughters. Their eldest son, <a href="/wiki/P._V._Ranga_Rao" title="P. V. Ranga Rao">P. V. Ranga Rao</a>, was the education minister in <a href="/wiki/Kotla_Vijaya_Bhaskara_Reddy" title="Kotla Vijaya Bhaskara Reddy">Kotla Vijaya Bhaskara Reddy</a>'s cabinet and an <a href="/wiki/Member_of_the_Legislative_Assembly_(India)" title="Member of the Legislative Assembly (India)">MLA</a> from <a href="/wiki/Hanamkonda_(Assembly_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hanamkonda (Assembly constituency)">Hanamakonda</a> Assembly Constituency, in Warangal District for two terms. The second son, <a href="/wiki/P._V._Rajeshwar_Rao" title="P. V. Rajeshwar Rao">P.&#160;V. Rajeshwar Rao</a>, was a <a href="/wiki/Member_of_parliament,_Lok_Sabha" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of parliament, Lok Sabha">Member of Parliament</a> of the <a href="/wiki/11th_Lok_Sabha" title="11th Lok Sabha">11th Lok Sabha</a> (15 May 1996 – 4 December 1997) from <a href="/wiki/Secunderabad_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Secunderabad (Lok Sabha constituency)">Secunderabad Lok Sabha constituency</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-raj1_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-raj1-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-raj2_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-raj2-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The third son is P.V. Prabhakara Rao.
</p><p>The five daughters of P.V. Narasimha Rao are Smt. N. Sharada Devi, wife of Sri N. Venkata Krishna Rao; Smt. K. Saraswathi Devi, wife of K. Sarath Chandra Rao; Smt. S. Vani Devi, wife of Sri S. Divakara Rao; Smt. Vijaya Somayaji, wife of Sri Ramakrishna Somayaji; and Smt. K. Jaya Devi, wife of Sri K. Revathi Nandan.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biographical_and_political_evaluation">Biographical and political evaluation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Biographical and political evaluation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On the occasion of 25 years of <a href="/wiki/Economic_liberalisation_in_India" title="Economic liberalisation in India">economic liberalisation in India</a>, there have been several books published by authors, journalists and civil servants evaluating Rao's contributions.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> While Vinay Sitapati's book <i>Half Lion: How P.V. Narasimha Rao transformed India</i> (2016) gives a renewed biographical picture of his entire life,<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Baru" class="mw-redirect" title="Sanjay Baru">Sanjay Baru</a>'s book <i>1991: How P V Narasimha Rao made history</i> (2016)<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Jairam_Ramesh" title="Jairam Ramesh">Jairam Ramesh</a>'s book From the brink to back: India's 1991 story (2015)<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> focusses on his role in unleashing the reforms in the year 1991 as the Prime Minister of India.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literary_achievements">Literary achievements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Literary achievements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Rao's mother tongue was <a href="/wiki/Telugu_language" title="Telugu language">Telugu</a>, and he had an excellent command of <a href="/wiki/Marathi_language" title="Marathi language">Marathi</a>. In addition to eight other Indian languages (<a href="/wiki/Bengali_language" title="Bengali language">Bengali</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gujarati_language" title="Gujarati language">Gujarati</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kannada" title="Kannada">Kannada</a>, <a href="/wiki/Malayalam" title="Malayalam">Malayalam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Odia_language" title="Odia language">Odia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit">Sanskrit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil</a> and <a href="/wiki/Urdu" title="Urdu">Urdu</a>), he spoke English, French, Arabic, Spanish, German and <a href="/wiki/Persian_language" title="Persian language">Persian</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Telegraph_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Telegraph-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> He was able to speak 17 languages.<sup id="cite_ref-PVN_–_Obituary_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PVN_–_Obituary-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-&#39;PV&#39;:_A_scholar,_a_statesman_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-&#39;PV&#39;:_A_scholar,_a_statesman-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Due to his college education in <a href="/wiki/Fergusson_College" title="Fergusson College">Fergusson College</a> in <a href="/wiki/Pune" title="Pune">Pune</a>, then an affiliated college of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Mumbai" title="University of Mumbai">University of Mumbai</a> (but now with <a href="/wiki/Pune_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Pune University">Pune University</a>), he became a very prolific reader and speaker of Marathi.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_26-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He translated the great Telugu literary work <a href="/wiki/Veyipadagalu" class="mw-redirect" title="Veyipadagalu">Veyipadagalu</a> of <i>Kavi Samraat</i> <a href="/wiki/Viswanatha_Satyanarayana" title="Viswanatha Satyanarayana">Viswanatha Satyanarayana</a> into Hindi as <i>Sahasraphan</i>. He also translated Hari Narayan Apte's Marathi novel <i><a href="/wiki/Pan_Lakshat_Kon_Gheto" title="Pan Lakshat Kon Gheto">Pan Lakshat Kon Gheto</a></i> (But Who Pays Attention?) into Telugu. He was also invited to be the chief guest of Akhil Bhartiya Marathi Sahitya Sanmelan where he gave speech in Marathi.
</p><p>In his later life he wrote his autobiography, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Insider_(Rao_novel)" title="The Insider (Rao novel)">The Insider</a></i>, which depicts his experiences in politics.
</p>
<blockquote>
<p>"Sonia Gandhi praised contributions of all Congress prime ministers except P. V. Narasimha Rao in her speech ... Making no mention of Rao in her 15-minute speech, she said Rajiv Gandhi scripted the course of economic policies that were followed by the government (headed by Rao) for the following five years."
</p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p>"Even today, the Congress leadership shows extreme reluctance to acknowledge the role P. V. Narasimha Rao played in appointing <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> as his finance minister and giving him the freedom to unveil the economic reforms package to bail the Indian economy out of an unprecedented crisis. The Congress leadership was correct in blaming Narasimha Rao for his political misjudgment on the <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya_dispute" title="Ayodhya dispute">Ayodhya issue</a>. But it is now time the same leadership also acknowledged Narasimha Rao's role in ushering in economic reforms."
</p>
</blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Centenary_celebrations">Centenary celebrations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Centenary celebrations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In June 2020, <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Telangana" title="Government of Telangana">Government of Telangana</a>, led by <a href="/wiki/Telangana_Rashtra_Samithi" class="mw-redirect" title="Telangana Rashtra Samithi">Telangana Rashtra Samithi</a> has declared to organise one-year long centenary celebrations of Rao. The state government also decided to set up a memorial and five bronze statues at various places, including <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad" title="Hyderabad">Hyderabad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Warangal" title="Warangal">Warangal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Karimnagar" title="Karimnagar">Karimnagar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vangara,_Warangal_Urban_district" class="mw-redirect" title="Vangara, Warangal Urban district">Vangara</a> and Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: In popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the year 2019, an independent biographical documentary film named <i>P V: Change with Continuity</i> (2019) directed and produced by Sravani Kotha and Srikar Reddy Gopaladinne released on the streaming platform <a href="/wiki/Vimeo" title="Vimeo">Vimeo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The documentary features rare archival footage and interviews of several distinguished people closely related to Rao's life and work.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Suresh Kumar appeared as Rao in the 2019 film <i><a href="/wiki/NTR:_Mahanayakudu" title="NTR: Mahanayakudu">NTR: Mahanayakudu</a></i> directed by <a href="/wiki/Krish_(director)" class="mw-redirect" title="Krish (director)">Krish</a> which chart the life of the Indian actor-politician <a href="/wiki/N._T._Rama_Rao" title="N. T. Rama Rao">N. T. Rama Rao</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The same year, Ajit Satbhai portrayed Rao as the former Prime Minister of India in the film <a href="/wiki/The_Accidental_Prime_Minister_(film)" title="The Accidental Prime Minister (film)"><i>The Accidental Prime Minister</i></a> by <a href="/wiki/Vijay_Gutte" title="Vijay Gutte">Vijay Gutte</a>, about <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Pradhanmantri" title="Pradhanmantri">Pradhanmantri</a></i> (<abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Prime Minister&#39;), a 2013 Indian docudrama television series which aired on <a href="/wiki/ABP_News" title="ABP News">ABP News</a> and covers the various policies and political tenures of Indian PMs, based the twentieth episode - "P. V. Narasimha Rao and Corruption charges against him" - on his term as the country's leader; <a href="/wiki/Ravi_Jhankal" title="Ravi Jhankal">Ravi Jhankal</a> portrayed the role of Rao.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Awards">Awards</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=P._V._Narasimha_Rao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Awards">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Rao was awarded the Pratibha Murthy Lifetime Achievement Award.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Many people across the party line supported the name of P.&#160;V. Narasimha Rao for <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a>. <a href="/wiki/Telangana" title="Telangana">Telangana</a> Chief Minister <a href="/wiki/K._Chandrashekhar_Rao" class="mw-redirect" title="K. Chandrashekhar Rao">K. Chandrashekhar Rao</a> supported the move to give Bharat Ratna to Rao.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> BJP leader <a href="/wiki/Subramanian_Swamy" title="Subramanian Swamy">Subramanian Swamy</a> supported the move to give Bharat Ratna to Rao.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Sanjay_Baru" class="mw-redirect" title="Sanjay Baru">Sanjay Baru</a>, PM <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> wanted to give Bharat Ratna to Rao during his tenure but failed.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2020, <a href="/wiki/Telangana_Legislative_Assembly" title="Telangana Legislative Assembly">Telangana Legislative Assembly</a> adopted a resolution seeking to confer Bharat Ratna on Rao. The resolution also requested the Central Government to rename the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Hyderabad" title="University of Hyderabad">University of Hyderabad</a> after him.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><br />
</p>
